# LMFDB targets
<!-- tauceti-targets:v1 -->
Goal: Tau Ceti definitions and theorems for what the LMFDB displays and searches on, so that every LMFDB
definition becomes a Lean definition with the theory that makes it well defined. Twenty-three LMFDB roadmaps
are drafted but not yet merged (listed under Gaps); until they merge, this list holds what they consume: the
milestones of merged roadmaps that the drafts cite, the milestones that directly define something the LMFDB
displays, and their prerequisites inside each roadmap — restricted to what is not yet on Tau Ceti main
(`977692b6`, 2026-10-24). Done items appear only where an open item needs them.
Every item is a milestone its roadmap README states. Prove it in the generality the README states: never a
shortcut that works only for the stored LMFDB examples or a labelled instance.
Ordering rule: within each area, attempt the first open `[ ]` item whose `needs` have all landed (`[x]`),
treating `[~]` items as not yet landed. Slugs are the milestone's principal declaration in kebab-case; `needs`
refers to slugs in this file from any area. `serves` names the LMFDB drafts that consume the item, by
directory name, or `lmfdb <section>` where no draft cites it.
GlobalQuadraticForms' Hasse–Minkowski milestones are deliberately absent: they are under the hold
TauCetiRoadmap#525 until 2026-12-31.

## EllipticCurves
- [x] `tate-uniformization` — Layer 4, The Tate curve: "the Tate curve `E_q` and its uniformisation — pinned as the **point-level, Galois-equivariant statement** … with the subgroup `q^ℤ`, the uniformisation map, its kernel and surjectivity all named milestones. The coefficients are explicit `q`-series **defined over `ℤ⟦q⟧` first** … Named outputs: `Δ[q]`, `j[q] = 1/q + 744 + ⋯`, `v[Δ] = v[q]`, and the split-multiplicative-reduction theorem for `E_q`." (serves: AdelicImagesAndModularCurves; needs: none; landed elsewhere: TauCeti/AlgebraicGeometry/EllipticCurve/TateCurve/Uniformization.lean:44 `tateUniformization` — `Kˣ ⧸ zpowers q → E_q[K]` for complete ultrametric `K` and `‖q‖ < 1`, identity coset to infinity :51, inverse image of infinity exactly `q^ℤ` :63, respects inversion :96, commutes with isometric embeddings :116; Equation.lean:309 `equation_tateQuotientCoordinates`; Specialization.lean:71 `tateCurveAt`, Basic.lean:69 `tateCurve` over `ℤ⟦X⟧` with :108 `twelve_mul_tateCurve_a₆` the division by 12 done in `ℤ⟦q⟧` and :218 `exists_tateCurve_j_eq`; Reduction.lean:71 `valuation_tateCurveAt_Δ` and :88 `hasSplitMultiplicativeReduction_tateCurveAt`)
> The file's own docstring: "These statements do not assert additivity, injectivity away from infinity, or surjectivity, and the map is not yet bundled as a group homomorphism." Those are the two open items below; coverage.md's "L^×/q^ℤ ≅ E_q(L) remaining" is right for exactly that part.
- [ ] `tate-uniformization-mul` — Layer 4, The Tate curve: "for each finite extension `L/K` [complete, or completed], a group isomorphism `L^× / q^ℤ ≅ E_q[L]`" — the homomorphism half: `tateUniformization q hq [x * y] = tateUniformization q hq x + tateUniformization q hq y`, bundled as an `AddMonoidHom` out of `Additive [Lˣ ⧸ zpowers q]`, hence injective by the landed kernel. (serves: AdelicImagesAndModularCurves; needs: `tate-uniformization`)
> Route as the README fixes it: an identity of `q`-series [ATAEC V.3.1, Tate 1995], proved on the fundamental annulus the way TateCurve/Equation.lean proves the Weierstrass equation, from the formal Tate point in `ℤ⟦u⟧⟦v⟧` of TateCurve/FormalPoint.lean. No rigid-space quotient and no Mathlib `℘`.
- [ ] `tate-uniformization-surjective` — Layer 4, The Tate curve: "its kernel and surjectivity all named milestones … compatible in `L` and Galois-equivariant": surjectivity of `tateUniformization` onto `E_q[L]`, and the bundled isomorphism `tateUniformizationEquiv : Lˣ ⧸ zpowers q ≃+ E_q[L]` for every complete finite extension `L/K`, equivariant under `Gal[L/K]`. (serves: AdelicImagesAndModularCurves; needs: `tate-uniformization-mul`)
> Compatibility in `L` and Galois equivariance are `mapAlong_tateUniformization` [Uniformization.lean:116] for the inclusion `L ⊆ L'` and for `σ ∈ Gal(L/K)`, each an isometry of complete `L`; what is new here is surjectivity and the bundle. AdelicImages 1629 reads the `G_{ℚ_2}`-module `E[P]` off this isomorphism, which also needs the converse that a curve with multiplicative reduction *is* a Tate curve or its twist — not a README milestone; see Gaps.

The analytic hypothesis must identify a continuation of the **actual Dirichlet series**: an analytic function on a connected open set containing both `s = 1` and a part of the half-plane of convergence, agreeing with the series there … the order of vanishing `r`, the leading coefficient `L^{[r]}[1]/r!`, the finite support of `∏_p c_p`, the real-period convention … and `Ш[E/ℚ]` finiteness as hypothesis — the rank equality and the leading-coefficient identity against the quotient above. **Proving** anything about it stays out of scope." EC-Periods 3.4 drafts `IsContinuation W U F` and 3.7 `WeakBSD`/`StrongBSD` over any number field; this item is their `K = ℚ` instance and must be the same declarations or be compared to them [Gaps].

- [ ] `kummer-cokernel-index` — Layer 7, Stretch, Cassels' prerequisites: "the local Kummer-cokernel indices" (serves: EllipticCurvePeriodsAndModularParametrizations; needs: `selmer-group-isogeny`)
> For each place `v` of `K` and isogeny `φ : E → E'`, the index `[E'(K_v) : φ E(K_v)]` — the order of the image of the local Kummer map of `φ` in `H¹(K_v, E[φ])` — is finite; it is `1` at a finite place of good reduction not dividing `deg φ`, and is computed at the real place and at primes dividing `deg φ` or the discriminant [Cassels 1965 §§3–4; Milne, *Arithmetic Duality Theorems* I.7]. Its finiteness and finite support are what make the product formula below a finite product.
- [ ] `global-duality-product-formula` — Layer 7, Stretch, Cassels' prerequisites: "**local Tate duality** and the global duality/product formula the proof uses" (serves: EllipticCurvePeriodsAndModularParametrizations; needs: `kummer-cokernel-index`, `selmer-group-isogeny`, `tate-duality-pairing`; lookahead: no — Tau Ceti main has no global Poitou–Tate duality or product formula for Selmer groups, and local Tate duality is ClassFieldTheory's `tateDualityPairing` on `GalRep` coefficients, which needs an `E[φ]` adapter)
> The target is Cassels' lemma `#Sel_φ(E/K) / #Sel_φ̂(E'/K) = ∏_v [E'(K_v) : φE(K_v)] / #E'(K)[φ̂]…`-shape, [Cassels 1965 Theorem 1.1; Milne ADT I.7.3], the single place where the global duality enters. Local Tate duality on main: TauCeti/NumberTheory/ClassFieldTheory/Local/Duality/Basic.lean:385 `tateDualityPairing`, perfectness Perfect.lean:242 `tateDualityPairing_perfect`. The `E[φ]`-with-`E[φ̂]`-as-Cartier-dual identification is the Weil-pairing material of Isogeny/Dual/WeilPairing.lean.

- [ ] `serre-duality-residue-pairing` — Layer 10B [iv]: "meromorphic differentials and their divisors, and Serre duality by the residue pairing [Forster §17]: H¹[𝒪_D]^* ≅ H⁰[Ω_{−D}], whence ℓ[D] − ℓ[K−D] = deg D + 1 − g, dim H⁰[Ω¹] = g, deg K = 2g − 2, and the vanishing H¹[𝒪_D] = 0 for deg D > 2g − 2" (serves: CanonicalModelsAndGonality, GroupActionsOnRiemannSurfaces, NoncongruenceModularForms; needs: `analytic-riemann-roch`)
- [ ] `genus-formula-congruence-subgroup` — Layer 10B: "Applying the shared Riemann–Hurwitz theorem to X[Γ] → X[1] with the Fuchsian elliptic and cusp local-multiplicity formulas gives g = 1 + d/12 − ε₂/4 − ε₃/3 − ε∞/2, where d = [PSL₂[ℤ] : Γ̄], not [SL₂[ℤ] : Γ]" (serves: AdelicImagesAndModularCurves, NoncongruenceModularForms, CanonicalModelsAndGonality, lmfdb cmf.dimension; needs: `degree-compactified-quotient-map-map-sl2z-to-psl2r`, `fuchsian-genus`, `riemann-hurwitz`)
> `fuchsian-genus` and `riemann-hurwitz` are FuchsianOrbifolds Layer 5 [open there; see Cross-area needs]. The Fuchsian local multiplicities at elliptic points and cusps are on main (TauCeti/Analysis/Complex/Fuchsian/Compactification/{Elliptic,Cusp}/LocalMultiplicity.lean), as are the explicit counts for Γ₀[N]: ε∞ = Σ_{g ∣ N} φ[gcd[g, N/g]] `card_cuspOrbit_Gamma0` (TauCeti/NumberTheory/ModularForms/CongruenceSubgroups/Cusp/Count.lean:252), ε₂ and ε₃ (CongruenceSubgroups/Elliptic/Gamma0.lean), no elliptic points for Γ₁[N ≥ 4] (Elliptic/Gamma1.lean). The genus of X₀[N] on LMFDB modular-curve pages and dim S₂ = g on weight-2 newspace pages are the displayed values.
- [ ] `analytic-genus-eq-topological-genus` — Layer 10B [v]: "Define the analytic genus g_an[X] := dim_ℂ H¹[X, 𝒪_X] and prove the named comparison theorem RiemannSurface.analyticGenus_eq_topologicalGenus : g_an[X] = RiemannSurface.genus X" via deg K_X = 2g_an − 2 and the analytic Riemann–Hurwitz equality for a finite meromorphic map (serves: NoncongruenceModularForms, LMFDBLabelsAndCompleteness, GroupActionsOnRiemannSurfaces; needs: `serre-duality-residue-pairing`, `riemann-hurwitz`, `fuchsian-genus`, `degree-compactified-quotient-map-map-sl2z-to-psl2r`)

Drop this and the two items above if the merged list is to stay strictly draft-driven. ## BelyiMaps
> The merged README's "Merge boundary and staged successors" table assigns Layers 7–8 to a successor roadmap `BelyiAnalyticCovers`, Layers 9–11 to `BelyiAlgebraicAndDescent`, and Layers 12–14 to `BelyiArithmeticActions`, none of which is a roadmap directory on main; each such layer opens "no milestone of this layer is a declaration or a completion claim of this PR". The fleet has nonetheless landed Layer 7 and most of Layer 8 under focus `BelyiMaps` [open #17054 is Layer 8.4], so the Layer 8 and Layer 14 milestones the drafts cite are listed here; the Layer 9–11 prerequisites of 14.2–14.3 are not itemized and go under Gaps.

ArtinRepresentations 1.1 only records the comparison `toContinuousRep`/`ofContinuousRep` with its own `ArtinRep`, so the carrier alone is the demand.

## StableReduction
> HyperellipticCurves consumes Layers 0–8 for `genus2reduction`, the special fibre of the minimal regular model and
> its dual graph, the semistability criterion and Liu's reduction types; CanonicalModelsAndGonality 9.5 consumes a
> smooth proper model only. Layers 9–11 [pointed reduction, stable maps] are cited by no draft and are left out,
> as are the clutching constructors, automorphism finiteness and the Jacobian criterion of Layers 3 and 7. The
> JacobianChallenge contracts J-A1, J-A2, J-B, J-C, J-D, J-E are that roadmap's milestones; see Cross-area needs.

(serves: HyperellipticCurves; needs: none; landed elsewhere: TauCeti/AlgebraicGeometry/Curves/Family.lean:56 `FamilyOfCurves` — proper, flat, `LocallyOfFinitePresentation`, relative dimension at most one; `of_isPullback`, `isZariskiLocalAtTarget`, the field case at :133)

## SchemeAndStackFoundations
> Demanded sections: §0.3, §0.11 and §2.6 by CanonicalModelsAndGonality 9.2–9.4; §3.2's degree bounds, §3.3's
> singular Riemann–Roch and §3.7's Abel maps by CanonicalModelsAndGonality 2.5, 4.4, 9.2, 7.x; §5.3–5.6 by
> CanonicalModelsAndGonality 5.3; §5.9–5.10 by HilbertModularSurfaces throughout. §0.29's demand
> [AbelianVarietiesOverFiniteFields: `qPowerFrobenius` and its naturality] is on main,
> `TauCeti/AlgebraicGeometry/Frobenius/Iterate.lean:149,175`, and nothing open needs it, so it has no item.
> Prerequisites are itemized down to the sections the README's *Needs* lines name, with two cuts along the
> README's own theorem boundaries: §3.7 is itemized only as `abelMaps_highDegree` [its `picardStack`/`sectionStack`
> carrier and hence §§1.18–1.19 are consumed by no draft], and §3.4 only as its Cartier/Weil comparisons [its
> Hilbert-90 description and hence §2.11 likewise]. §3.3's `curve_serre_duality` and §3.2's Riemann–Hurwitz, projective-line
> and genus-one theorems are cited by no draft and have no item. Section numbers are the README's.
- [ ] `relative-proj-twisting-sheaf` — Layer 0, §0.3 Proj, base change and the relative Proj: "Construct the quasi-coherent twisting sheaves `O[n]`, `n ∈ ℤ`, with the graded map `A → ⊕_{n ≥ 0} [relativeProj.toBase]_* O[n]` ... *Generated in degree one* means generated by `A_1` as a graded `A_0`-algebra; then `O[1]` is invertible and multiplication gives `O[n] ≅ O[1]^{⊗n}`" (serves: CanonicalModelsAndGonality, HyperellipticCurves, HilbertModularSurfaces; needs: `relative-proj`; partial: #21367, #21478, #21533, #21661, #21762, #21926)
> `relative-proj` is the StableReduction `[x]` above: main's `CommMon.relativeProj` is the README's construction for an arbitrary graded quasi-coherent algebra, so `relativeProj_eq_stableReduction` is moot. `Proj.isPullback_baseChange` is on main as `AlgebraicGeometry.Proj.isPullback_map_of_isBaseChange` in `TauCeti/AlgebraicGeometry/ProjectiveSpectrum/BaseChange.lean`, and `relativeProj_affine_iso` is the chart over an affine base in `RelativeProj/Basic.lean`.

- [ ] `relative-proj-veronese` — Layer 0, §0.3: "for `d ≥ 1` the Veronese subalgebra `A^{[d]} = ⊕_k A_{dk}`, which has the same degree-zero part `A_0`, satisfies `relativeProj A^{[d]} ≅ relativeProj A` over `S`, with `O[n]` corresponding to `O[dn]`" (serves: HyperellipticCurves; needs: `relative-proj-twisting-sheaf`)

- [ ] `relative-proj-closed-immersion` — Layer 0, §0.3: "if `A_1` is of finite type and `A` is generated in degree one, the graded map `Sym_{O_S} A_1 → A` is surjective in every positive degree and induces a closed immersion `relativeProj A → relativeProj [Sym_{O_S} A_1]` over `S` pulling `O[1]` back to `O[1]` ..., so `relativeProj A → S` is projective." (serves: CanonicalModelsAndGonality, HyperellipticCurves, HilbertModularSurfaces; needs: `relative-proj-twisting-sheaf`)

- [ ] `is-hermite-normal-form` — Layer 3, 3.5 Hermite normal form [column-style, upper]: "An m × k integer matrix H is in Hermite normal form if there is a strictly increasing map p … the pivot rows … `H_{p[j], j} > 0`; `H_{i, j} = 0` for i > p[j] …; and for every column j′ > j to its right, `0 ≤ H_{p[j], j′} < H_{p[j], j}`", with `IntMatrix.IsHermiteNormalForm`, `pivot_unique`, `linearIndependent_cols`, `isUpperTriangular`, `det_eq_prod_diag`, `one` (serves: LMFDBLabelsAndCompleteness; needs: none)
> Convention pinned by the README: columns generate the lattice, pivots are the lowest nonzero entries, upper triangle; Cohen/Belabas as used for LMFDB ideal labels (expansion_plan/work/lmfdb.md line 187).

> The README pins no API name for 3.26; the slug is descriptive.

Then `Φ[P] := g[P]^m` lies in `𝓞_K`. Extend Φ multiplicatively to nonzero ideals A prime to m", with `stickelbergerPhi`, `_prime`, `_mul`, `_top`, `_norm_sq`, `_galois`, `_modEq` (serves: CharacterSumsOverFiniteFields, HypergeometricMotives; needs: `power-residue-symbol`, `power-residue-symbol-ideal`; partial: #21522, #21826)

- [ ] `span-stickelberger-phi` — Layer 1, 1.55 Stickelberger's relation: "the principal ideal generated by `Φ[P] = g[P]^m` is `P^γ`, where `γ = Σ_{1 ≤ t < m, gcd[t, m] = 1} t σ_t⁻¹ ∈ ℤ[Gal[K/ℚ]]` … Consequently `[Φ[A]] = A^γ` for every ideal A prime to m, and `[Φ[α]] = [α^γ]` for α prime to m", including the prime `p = 2` case the sources omit (serves: CharacterSumsOverFiniteFields, HypergeometricMotives; needs: `stickelberger-phi`, `power-residue-symbol`)
> The digit-sum valuation `v_𝔓̃(g(ω^{−α})) = s(α)` that HypergeometricMotives 810 and CharacterSumsOverFiniteFields 755 cite is a proof step here and the exported statement of 7.12 below.
- [ ] `stickelberger-congruence` — Layer 7, 7.12 Stickelberger's finite Gauss-sum valuation: "If 0≤a<q−1 has digits `a_i`, put `s=Σa_i` and `h=∏a_i!`. Then `h·[−gaussSum[ω⁻ᵃ,ψ]]≡[ζ_p−1]ˢ mod Pˢ⁺¹`. Thus `v_P[gaussSum]=s` when `v_P[ζ_p−1]=1`, and the valuation with `v_p[p]=1` is s/[p−1]" (serves: CharacterSumsOverFiniteFields, HypergeometricMotives; needs: none)
> LMFDB `hgm`: the integrality `q^λ H_q ∈ ℤ̄` of hypergeometric sums (HypergeometricMotives 4.9) is this valuation summed over the Gauss-sum factors. Needs only Mathlib's `gaussSum`, `gaussSum_mul`, `gaussSum_one_left`.

For a family of flat central scalar maps jointly detecting A-module exactness, projectivity descends when it holds after every scalar change" (serves: AbelianVarietiesOverFiniteFields, QuaternionArithmetic, FiniteGroupInvariants; needs: `balanced-extension-noncommutative-orders`)

## NewtonPolygons
> No STATUS.md; checked on main. `TauCeti/NumberTheory/NewtonPolygon/{Basic,Slopes,Mul,Roots,Irreducible}.lean` over `TauCeti/Analysis/Convex/Sequence/*` cover Layer 0, §2.1 (`Polynomial.newtonPolygon`, `PowerSeries.newtonPolygon`, scalar/`X^k`/rescale/reverse/map laws, Convention 4's endpoint and cardinality statements `newtonPolygon_natTrailingDegree`, `card_newtonSlopes`), §3.2 (`newtonPolygon_mul` Mul.lean:220, `newtonSlopes_mul` :261), §3.3 (`newtonSlopes_eq_map_roots` Roots.lean:87, `newtonSlopes_eq_map_roots_map` :99, face counts :133–163) and §6.1 (`irreducible_of_forall_mem_newtonSlopes_eq_div` Irreducible.lean:115, `den_dvd_natDegree_of_dvd` :60, `den_dvd_card_newtonSlopes` Slopes.lean:242). §2.2 is in flight (#20274, ldct) but no LMFDB draft cites it. CurvesOverFiniteFields' demand (Conventions 1–4, §2.1, §3.2, §3.3) is therefore met on main and carries no items; PadicExtensionFamilies' demand is met except §6.2.

## IntegralLattices

## MassFormula
> Demand: LMFDBLabelsAndCompleteness [Layer 0, Layer 4: Theorems 1 and 2, the dichotomy, the orbit
> count, the tame count], PadicExtensionFamilies [Layers 0, 3, 4: σ_K[n], c, the Eisenstein region and
> its measure, the root-count integral, Theorems 1 and 2, the orbit count]. LMFDB: the completeness of
> the p-adic fields table [`lf.*`] rests on Serre's count. Layers 0–2 and the orbit count are on main;
> so are the reductions of Theorem 2, the finite half of the dichotomy and the tame count to Theorem 1
> [TauCeti/NumberTheory/LocalField/MassFormula/Representative.lean:166, Finite.lean:107, :131].
- [ ] `eisenstein-separable-ae` — Layer 3, Almost every Eisenstein polynomial is separable: "the non-separable locus is null. In equal characteristic this is where the inseparable polynomials are discarded, and it is the reason the count is over subextensions of SeparableClosure K" (serves: PadicExtensionFamilies, LMFDBLabelsAndCompleteness; needs: none; stalled: #18121 — @sqrt-of-2's PR has merge conflicts, no commit since 2026-10-24)
- [ ] `aemeasurable-root-count` — Layer 3, Almost-everywhere measurability of the root count: "For each L ∈ σ_K n, state AEMeasurable [fun a => [rootCount L a : ℝ≥0∞]] [μ.restrict [eisensteinSet K n]]" (serves: PadicExtensionFamilies, LMFDBLabelsAndCompleteness; needs: none; stalled: #19315 — @tauceti-ai-for-science's PR has merge conflicts, no commit since 2026-10-24)
- [ ] `local-fibre-count` — Layer 3, The local fibre count: "for L ∈ σ_K n with Eisenstein generator ξ of minimal polynomial f, and d = d L … and for every ρ with d + n ≤ n · ρ: a monic g of degree n over 𝒪[K] has a root in the cube ξ + π^ρ · 𝒪_L … if and only if addVal [g ξ] ≥ n · ρ + d, and then exactly one" (serves: PadicExtensionFamilies; needs: none; stalled: #18121 — @sqrt-of-2's PR has merge conflicts, no commit since 2026-10-24)
- [ ] `lintegral-root-count` — Layer 3, The integral of the root count: "for each L ∈ σ_K n, ∫_{E_n} rootCount L = q^{−[d L + 1]} · [1 − q^{−1}]. The change of variables runs in power-basis coordinates: a ball of the ring of integers is the ideal generated by a suitable element, in coordinates the image lattice of the matrix of multiplication by it" (serves: PadicExtensionFamilies, LMFDBLabelsAndCompleteness; needs: `local-fibre-count`, `aemeasurable-root-count`, `eisenstein-separable-ae`)
> #21087 [open, gqa] writes the root count of an Eisenstein polynomial as a finite sum of ball indicators with the balls fixed independently of the polynomial; it is a step of this integral, not the integral.
- [ ] `tsum-one-div-nat-card-residue-field-pow-wild-exponent` — Layer 4, Theorem 1: "for 0 < n, ∑' L : σ_K n, 1 / q ^ c L = n in ℝ≥0∞. Integrate the root-count identity of Layer 3 over E_n, interchange the sum and integral using MeasureTheory.lintegral_tsum with Layer 3's countability and almost-everywhere measurability targets, and divide by the measure of the region" (serves: PadicExtensionFamilies, LMFDBLabelsAndCompleteness, lmfdb lf completeness; needs: `lintegral-root-count`, `aemeasurable-root-count`, `eisenstein-separable-ae`)
> Countability [Countable.lean:45], the region's measure [Eisenstein/Region.lean:189] and the pointwise sum [RootCount.lean:78 `tsum_rootCount_eq_of_separable`] are on main.
- [ ] `totally-ramified-of-degree-infinite-iff` — Layer 4, The finiteness dichotomy, the finite direction: "σ_K n is infinite if and only if K has equal characteristic p and p ∣ n. Both directions are targets. The forward direction follows from Theorem 1 together with the uniform bound c L ≤ n · natCastValuation K n h" (serves: LMFDBLabelsAndCompleteness, PadicExtensionFamilies; needs: `tsum-one-div-nat-card-residue-field-pow-wild-exponent`)

- BianchiModularForms:171 wants the Mordell–Weil data of a curve over an imaginary quadratic `K` — "`rank`, `torsion`, `regulator`, `omega`, `sha`, `tamagawa`, the BSD formula" — from EllipticCurves. The merged README states the BSD quotient "pinned to **`K = ℚ`**" and says "the general-`K` BSD quotient therefore belongs to the complex-analytic successor roadmap"; `omega` over `K` and the formula over `K` are EllipticCurvePeriodsAndModularParametrizations 2.5 `globalPeriod` and 3.6 `bsdFactor`, not this area's. Rank, torsion, regulator, Tamagawa numbers and `Ш` over `K` are on main for any number field.
- EllipticCurvePeriodsAndModularParametrizations 3.4 and 3.7 define `IsContinuation W U F`, `WeakBSD W`, `StrongBSD W` over any number field while its line 160 lists "the BSD statement over `ℚ`" as EllipticCurves' [Layer 7's "statement-only milestone: full BSD over `ℚ`"]. The same object has two owners at `K = ℚ`; neither README says which declaration the other consumes. Either `bsd-statement` is `StrongBSD` at `K = ℚ`, or one lemma compares the two spellings — the README's own rule for `AbcConjecture` against formal-conjectures.
- EllipticCurvePeriodsAndModularParametrizations:729 `StrongBSD.sq` needs "#Ш is a square when finite" [the Cassels–Tate pairing is alternating, Cassels 1962]. No merged roadmap states it; the README's Cassels theorem is the isogeny invariance of the quotient [Cassels 1965], a different theorem.
- EllipticCurvePeriodsAndModularParametrizations 3.6 `bsdFactor W` [the `Ш`-free part, over `K`] and the README's BSD quotient over `ℚ` [with `#Ш`] are the same quantity up to `#Ш` at `K = ℚ`; the draft asserts `bsdFactor_eq` against "the quotient over `ℚ`" of Layer 7, so `bsd-quotient` must expose `tamagawaProduct` and `Ω(E)` as separate declarations for that lemma to be stated.
- AdelicImagesAndModularCurves:1629 needs, for "the structure of `E[P]` as a `G_{ℚ_2}`-module: EC Layer 4's Tate curve", that a curve over a local field with split multiplicative reduction [`v(j) < 0`] is isomorphic to `E_q` for the unique `q` with `j(q) = j`, and a nonsplit one to its quadratic twist [ATAEC V.5.3 b, V.5.4]. The README's Tate-curve bullet states the forward direction only — "Named outputs: `Δ(q)`, `j(q) = 1/q + 744 + ⋯`, `v(Δ) = v(q)`, and the split-multiplicative-reduction theorem for `E_q`" — and cites V.5.3 as a source, not as a milestone. The converse needs the `q`-expansion of `j` to be invertible over the valuation ring and is a milestone of its own.
> > Naming, not a gap: the README's `EndOver K E` is `WeierstrassCurve.Affine.Hom W W` on main [Isogeny/Hom/Basic.lean:106, ring structure in Isogeny/Hom/Ring.lean], and `GeometricEnd`, `HasGeometricCM`, `CMAction` are Isogeny/Hom/ComplexMultiplication.lean:94, :100, :143. SatoTateGroups:1383 and AdelicImages:84 cite the README names.
> > Not a gap: the conductor ideal `𝔫_E` of Bianchi:151 and HilbertModularForms:1172 is main's `algorithmicConductor` over any Dedekind domain [TateAlgorithm/Reduction/Conductor.lean:103], which is how the knowl `ec.conductor` defines it [exponents from Tate's algorithm]; the algorithmic–Artin comparison is not needed for any LMFDB field.
- **Trace form.** LMFDBLabelsAndCompleteness:173 consumes "the Modular Forms roadmap's newforms, Galois conjugates, trace form, coefficient field". The ModularForms README defines no trace form: Layer 9 has "Hecke characteristic polynomials … its coefficients from traces of powers" and "Galois-conjugate forms and orbits", never the form Σ_σ f^σ with coefficients Tr_{K_f/ℚ}[a_n], nor the trace-vector ordering of orbits that the audit lists as a canonical normal form (expansion_plan/work/lmfdb.md:188). Needs a Layer 9 bullet; LMFDBLabels would then own only the ordering.
- **Dimension of S_k[N, χ] and of newspaces.** LMFDB newspace pages display dim S_k^new[N, χ] per character orbit (knowls cmf.dimension, cmf.newspace, cmf.dim_decomposition). The README's 10C states formulas only for M_k[Γ], S_k[Γ] of a group: "dim M_k[Γ] = [k−1][g−1] + ⌊k/4⌋·ε₂ + ⌊k/3⌋·ε₃ + [k/2]·ε∞"; no per-nebentypus formula [Cohen–Oesterlé] and no old/new Möbius inversion for dim S_k^new. LMFDBLabelsAndCompleteness:210 carries "the Cohen–Oesterlé dimension formula for S_k[N, χ] with nontrivial χ" as a *named hypothesis*, so no draft consumes it as a theorem, but the audit's `cmf` row (lmfdb.md:73) lists "spaces/dims" as LMFDB content and the per-character formula is how every displayed newspace dimension is produced.
- **Eisenstein newforms and newspaces.** The LMFDB has Eisenstein newforms with labels (knowls cmf.eisenstein_newform, cmf.eisenstein_newspace, cmf.eisenstein_label); the audit flags "Eisenstein newforms … (extend MF)" (lmfdb.md:73). The README's Layer 0 states the series E_k^{ψ,φ,t} and "the Eisenstein subspace they span" and nothing about an old/new decomposition of E_k[N, χ] or an Eisenstein newform.
- **Minimal twist.** Knowl cmf.minimal_twist and the audit's "minimal twists (extend MF)" (lmfdb.md:73): the README's Layer 9 defines twists, primitive associates and inner twists but no twist-minimal representative of a twist class.
- **Root number for non-self-dual newforms.** HalfIntegralWeightModularForms:111 consumes "Layer 7 [L[s, f], its functional equation and root number ε[f]]" and BianchiModularForms:141 "the Fricke sign [Layers 4, 6, 7 there]" on the general carrier `HeckeRing.GL2.Newform N k`. The README states a sign only where "𝒲_N f = ε·f — trivial nebentypus, or self-dual f = f_ρ"; for other f it gives the two-form equation "Λ_N[k − s, f] = i^k · Λ_N[s, g]" and says "a genuine FE sign beyond that needs f self-dual". LMFDB L-function pages display a complex root number for every newform. HIW:1004 uses ε[F] = −1, so HIW's F must be self-dual for the citation to resolve; the drafts should say so or the README should state the general ε = i^k·λ_N[f] against the conjugate L-function.
- **Scope of Layer 10 is congruence subgroups; NoncongruenceModularForms needs finite index.** README 10A: "This layer specializes that construction to congruence subgroups, identifies the cusps with Γ\ℙ¹[ℚ]"; NoncongruenceModularForms:959 reads "ModularForms Layer 10C's dimension formulas quantify over every finite-index Γ and are consumed", and 531/538 consume the genus formula for a noncongruence Γ̄. The 10C formulas are displayed for an unspecified Γ and their proof uses only finite index, so the fix is a README wording change in 10A/10C, not a draft change; until then the noncongruence consumption is unsupported by the text.
- **Hecke operators on M_k[Γ] for noncongruence Γ.** NoncongruenceModularForms:984 consumes "the Hecke operator T_p of ModularForms Layer 2 on M_k[Γ], defined by the double coset Γ !![1, 0; 0, p] Γ" for finite-index Γ. README Layer 2[b] defines "Tₙ, Tₚ as ℂ-linear endomorphisms of M_k[Γ₁[N]]" and the abstract ring for the congruence pairs only; the general-group double-coset operator is not a README statement [main's HeckeSlash files may already be general; the README should say so if they are].
- **Function-field dictionary.** GroupActionsOnRiemannSurfaces:163 attributes to 10B "the meromorphic function field ℳ[X] of a compact Riemann surface is a function field over ℂ of the same genus"; README 10B[iv] states "meromorphic differentials and their divisors, and Serre duality", not the function-field dictionary, which GARS itself assigns to AlgebraicCurves Layer 12 in the same sentence. Main has TauCeti/Analysis/Complex/RiemannSurface/FunctionField*.lean from another supplier.
- **Integral structures on forms.** Three demand lines need a lattice *of forms* with integral q-expansions from Layer 8 [EllipticCurvePeriodsAndModularParametrizations:917, NoncongruenceModularForms:1018–1019] or the identification of cuspidal symbols with H₁[X₀[N], ℤ] [EllipticCurvePeriods:753]; the README says "no lattice inside the space of forms is ever constructed" and the comparison "is **not** a milestone here". Listed as draft defects below; the gap is that no merged roadmap states a q-expansion principle or an integral structure on S_k itself, which 8W above also needs.
- QuaternionArithmetic 46–47, 171–176, 200, 941–948, 1339 consume "FuchsianOrbifolds Layer 7": L7.1 "the lattice `Γ(O) ≤ PSL₂(ℝ)` of the norm-one group of an order in an algebra split at exactly one real place, its discreteness, its finite covolume, and cocompactness exactly when `B` is division" and L7.2 "the maximal-order covolume formula `Area(Γ(O)\ℍ²) = 8π |d_k|^{3/2} ζ_k(2)/(4π²)^n ∏(N𝔭 − 1)`". The merged FuchsianOrbifolds README has Layers 0–6 and no arithmetic Fuchsian groups; its scope says "Congruence-subgroup arithmetic ... remain[s] with the modular-forms roadmap". Tau Ceti main's `Fuchsian/Arithmetic.lean` is only "Finite-index modular groups as cofinite Fuchsian groups". No merged roadmap states L7.1–L7.2.
- GroupActionsOnRiemannSurfaces 49, 134, 722, 1109 consume "FuchsianOrbifolds Layer 4a", a "minimum-area theorem [`π/21` for compact orientable hyperbolic orbifolds] ... for the Hurwitz bound's constant". FuchsianOrbifolds has no Layer 4a and no minimum-area statement; its 4.5 gives the area formula from the signature only. The Hurwitz bound itself is on main (TauCeti/FieldTheory/FunctionField/Automorphism/HurwitzBound.lean:83 `natCard_le_eighty_four_mul_genus_sub_one`), so the draft may not need the orbifold route.
- NoncongruenceModularForms 5.3 reads Poincaré's theorem as "for every Farey symbol `F`, `FareySymbol.polygon F` is a fundamental domain for `FareySymbol.group F` ... an instance of the Poincaré polygon theorem of FuchsianOrbifolds Layer 2.3, whose side-pairing hypotheses are exactly the label data" — the direction angle conditions ⇒ fundamental domain. FuchsianOrbifolds 2.3 lists "translated-interior no-overlap plus local finiteness" among its hypotheses, so the merged statement does not deliver the fundamental-domain conclusion from label data alone; the draft would have to prove no-overlap and local finiteness for the Farey polygon itself, or the roadmap's 2.3 be strengthened to the classical form.
- NoncongruenceModularForms 2.3 and AdelicImagesAndModularCurves 79 quote the genus formula `g = 1 + d/12 − ε₂/4 − ε₃/3 − ε∞/2` under FuchsianOrbifolds Layer 5 / "Layers 3–5". FuchsianOrbifolds states only the general Fuchsian Riemann–Hurwitz [5.6]; the counts `ε₂, ε₃, ε∞` for a congruence subgroup and the formula are ModularForms 10A/10B, which both drafts also cite.
- BelyiMaps 1.6 at degrees 6 to 11 needs transitive-group reference subgroups there; PolynomialGaloisGroups' README lists "Completeness of the classification of transitive subgroups in degrees 6 to 11" as out of scope and "makes no claim for degrees 6 to 11". LMFDBLabelsAndCompleteness 92 cites 1.6 as landed; it is landed for `n ≤ 5` only.
- BelyiMaps Layers 9–11 [9.1 algebraic Belyi pairs, 10.7 descent to `ℚ̄`, 11.3 the field of moduli, 11.5 fields of definition, 11.7 `trueOrbitSize c = [fieldOfModuli c : ℚ]`] are stated in the merged README but assigned to the successor roadmap `BelyiAlgebraicAndDescent`, "not a declaration or a completion claim of this PR", and untouched on main. They are the prerequisites of 14.2 and 14.3 [LMFDBLabelsAndCompleteness 92] and of NoncongruenceModularForms 6.3 and 7.4 [`X(Γ̄)` and `J` "defined over a number field `K`, the field of moduli of the dessin"]. Not itemized above.
- AdelicImagesAndModularCurves 1708 uses "JC / MC Layer 2 for the pairing at scheme level" on `A[ℓ]` for a principally polarised abelian surface. ModularCurves 2E constructs `e_N : E[N] ×_S E[N] ⟶ μ_N` for elliptic curves only; no merged roadmap states the Weil pairing on an abelian surface at scheme level [JacobianChallenge E is "polarizations and theta" and is partial].
- AdelicImagesAndModularCurves 72 reads ModularCurves Layer 10 as "the prime-level compactifications"; Layer 10 fixes its class as the diamond quotients `Y₁(N)/H` for `N ≥ 5` prime over `ℤ[1/N]`, so `X₀(N)` for composite `N` and `X(N)` are not in the merged statement.
- **JacobianChallenge ← CanonicalModelsAndGonality 781–784.** The draft needs "`θ` the principal polarization, `θ^g = g!` points", the Porteous class of `W^r_d` and the degeneracy-locus description of `W^r_d ⊆ Pic^d`. The README states theta ample and its Mumford homomorphism a principal polarization [Layer E, Polarizations and theta], nothing about `θ^g = g!` or Brill–Noether loci.
- **JacobianChallenge ← CanonicalModelsAndGonality 892.** "`W_d ⊆ Pic^d` is the scheme-theoretic image of `a_d`" for every `d`; the README constructs only `W_{g−1}` as a Cartier divisor. Lower `W_d` are not stated.
- **JacobianChallenge ← CanonicalModelsAndGonality 1103.** "its scheme-theoretic closure `𝒟 ⊆ 𝒳` is a relative effective Cartier divisor, finite flat of degree `d` over `R`" for a model `𝒳` over a DVR. Layer C's relative effective Cartier divisors are those of `X_T/T` for `X/k` a smooth proper curve and `T` a `k`-scheme; nothing over a DVR base. The relative-Jacobian roadmap in open PR #1232 is the natural owner.
- **JacobianChallenge ← AbelianVarietiesOverFiniteFields 905.** "`pRank A^∨ = pRank A` [Cartier duality]" cited to Layer E: the README's duality API gives `f^∨`, biduality and `[n]` as an isogeny, but no Cartier duality `A[n]^D ≅ A^∨[n]` or Weil pairing.
- **JacobianChallenge ← AbelianVarietiesOverFiniteFields 1045.** "`Jac C (K) = Pic⁰(C)(K)`, with Tau Ceti's `Divisor.degreeOneEquivDegreeZeroClassGroup`": the identification of `K`-points of the represented `Pic⁰` with the degree-zero divisor class group follows from representability, `rigidifiedPicardFunctorIso` and Layer A's dictionary, but no README sentence states it; a one-line bridge milestone would make the draft's citation exact.
- **ComplexTori ← GroupActionsOnRiemannSurfaces 169, 925.** The draft needs "the analytic Jacobian `H⁰(X,Ω)^∨ / H₁(X, ℤ)` and its comparison" with the algebraic `Jac X`. Milestone 1 builds `E/L` for a given full lattice `L` and its homomorphisms; that the period lattice of a compact Riemann surface is a full lattice in `H⁰(X,Ω)^∨` [Riemann's bilinear relations], and the comparison of `E/L` with the algebraic Jacobian [Abel's theorem, GAGA], appear in no merged README. ComplexCurveModuli D0.3 proves the genus-one case only.
- **ComplexCurveModuli ← GroupActionsOnRiemannSurfaces 594.** The draft needs "the uniformization of `X` by `ℍ/K`, `K = π₁(X)`" and "the identification of the deck group with `G`". D0.4 constructs a complete finite-area curvature −1 metric in the conformal class and says "No general uniformization or Hummel theorem is a property field or an external proof assumed available." The step from a complete hyperbolic metric to a biholomorphism `X ≅ ℍ/K` [Killing–Hopf for surfaces, or the Riemann mapping theorem for the universal cover] is in no merged README; FuchsianOrbifolds owns orbifold quotient uniformization of `ℍ` by a given group, not the converse.
- **ComplexKahlerGeometry ← HilbertModularSurfaces 64, 115, 752, 760.** Hodge symmetry on `X(Γ)` needs `X(Γ)` Kähler. The README puts the Fubini–Study form on `ℂPⁿ` in K7.2 and "complex submanifolds inherit Kähler metrics" in K6.0; the draft cites neither, and K7.2 carries a long tail of unconsumed computations, so a Kähler-projective-manifold milestone of its own would serve the draft better. Separately, HilbertModularSurfaces reads `h^{p,q}` both from K6.1 [analytic] and from SchemeAndStackFoundations §5.10 [algebraic surface theory]; the comparison between the two [GAGA for Hodge numbers] is in ComplexComparisonPartII, which is unmerged, and in no merged README.
- **Unmerged suppliers of the Kähler chain.** K1's eigenbundles, K2's local lemma and all of K5 consume SmoothBundlesConnections B2–B4, GlobalHolomorphicFunctions GH0, PseudoDifferentialOperators P1.0, VariationalMethods V2–V5.2 and UnboundedOperators UO-D06. None is a roadmap directory on main, so none can be a `needs:`; the affected items carry `lookahead: no`.
> What the LMFDB drafts need from these four areas that the merged READMEs do not state, or state with a different meaning. Never itemize.
> * **Minimal extension of the stable model.** HyperellipticCurves 3.7 defines the stable reduction type of a genus-2 curve as the type "of the geometric special fibre of its stable model over the minimal extension where it exists" and asks for "Liu's determination of the degree of the minimal extension `[L : K]`". StableReduction Layers 7–8 state existence "after a finite separable extension of degree at most `B_g = (2g − 2)(ℓ^(2g))!`", uniqueness over a fixed DVR, and "compatibility after a common finite extension"; no minimal extension, field of definition of the stable model, or its degree is stated. The LMFDB `g2c` reduction data need the minimal one.
> * **Artin conductor of the minimal regular model.** HyperellipticCurves 3.6 rests on "the regular models of the stable reduction roadmap Layer 5 (Liu's proof compares `Δ_min` with the Artin conductor of the minimal regular model)". StableReduction Layer 5 gives the model, `div(π) = Σ mᵢ[Cᵢ]`, "the intersection relations, adjunction, and genus formula"; it states no Artin conductor `−Art(X/R)`, no Euler characteristic of the special fibre against the generic one, and no conductor exponent. This is the `g2c.conductor` knowl's `N ∣ Δ_min`.
> * **Semistable versus nodal, as the draft needs it.** HyperellipticCurves 4.4 takes "semistability of the curve" to be "the stable reduction roadmap's predicate (a semistable model over `𝒪_K`)". StableReduction has two: `HasNodalReduction C R`, "some proper model is at worst nodal", and `SemistableFamily`, "geometric fibres of arithmetic genus at least one and no rational tails", and it forbids using "`semistable` as an unpinned synonym for `nodal`". Neither README states that for a smooth genus-`g ≥ 2` curve the two existence statements agree, which DDMM Theorem 1.8 implicitly uses; the draft should pin `HasNodalReduction` and prove the agreement itself or ask StableReduction for it.
> * **Good reduction as a predicate.** CanonicalModelsAndGonality 9.5 hypothesizes "a smooth proper model `𝒳 / R` of `X_F`". StableReduction's `Model` with `IsProper` and Mathlib's `Smooth` on `toBase` express this, but the README names no `HasGoodReduction` and states good reduction only as an acceptance example ("if `C` already extends to a smooth proper family ..., its stable reduction is that family itself"). Expressible; not named.
> * **Adjunction for reducible divisors.** HilbertModularSurfaces 2.5 and 4.4 apply "adjunction on the cycle of genus one" to the cusp cycle `Σ S_i` and read `K·S_i = b_i − 2` on its components. SchemeAndStackFoundations §5.10 states `adjunction` as "`2p_a(C) − 2 = C · (C + K)` for an integral curve `C ⊆ S`"; the effective-divisor form `p_a(D) = 1 + ½ D·(D + K)` for a reducible cycle is not stated, though Hartshorne V Ex. 1.3 gives it.
> * **Topological Euler number in Noether's formula.** HilbertModularSurfaces 4.7 reads `chi`, `h11`, `euler_nb`, `K2` from "`12χ = K² + c₂`" with "`e` the topological Euler number". SchemeAndStackFoundations §5.10 proves "`12 χ(O_S) = K² + deg c_2(T_S)`" and defers only the `ℓ`-adic comparison downstream ("the identification of `deg c_2(T_S)` with the ℓ-adic Euler characteristic ... is a downstream étale Gauss–Bonnet comparison"); the identification of `deg c_2(T_S)` with the topological Euler number of `S(ℂ)` for a smooth projective surface over `ℂ` is stated by no roadmap in this group, and ComplexKahlerGeometry K6.1, which the draft cites beside it, is a Hodge statement.
> * **Scheme-level smooth plane curve criterion.** CanonicalModelsAndGonality 9.4 wants "`IsSmooth G ↔ C'` smooth over `k` ↔ `φ_x : X_F → C'` an isomorphism" for `C' = V(G) ⊆ ℙ²_k`. AlgebraicCurves Layer 10 states the charts, "smoothness via homogeneous partial derivatives", and the function field; it does not state that smoothness of `V(G)` is equivalent to the normalization `X_F → V(G)` being an isomorphism, and over imperfect `k` the draft itself notes the right-hand side is regularity. The draft owns 9.4, so this is a gap only if AlgebraicCurves is meant to supply it.
- BianchiModularForms 148 asks ClassFieldTheory Layers 12–13 for "for a quadratic ray class character `ψ` of `K`, the quadratic extension `L_ψ/K` it cuts out, and the splitting dictionary `ψ(𝔭) = 1 ⟺ 𝔭` splits in `L_ψ`", and HilbertModularForms 975 for "the dictionary between quadratic Hecke characters and quadratic extensions ([Class Field Theory] Layer 12, the global correspondence)". The README's correspondence is `globalClassFieldCorrespondence : GlobalNormSubgroups K ≃o AbelianLayer G_K`, stated on open finite-index subgroups of `C_K`, and its reciprocity on characters is `rayClassArtinMap 𝔪 h𝔪 : RayClassGroup 𝔪 →* Gal(L/K)` with `rayClassArtinMap_idealClass`; no milestone names the field cut out by a ray-class or Hecke character, nor states `ψ(𝔭) = 1 ⟺ 𝔭 splits`. The field is `classField K (globalClassField K N)` for `N` the preimage of `ker ψ` under `GlobalNumberFields.rayClassQuotient 𝔪`, and the dictionary is `rayClassArtinMap_idealClass` plus `globalArtinMap_isArithFrobAt`; a one-paragraph milestone naming that composite would discharge both drafts.
- ArtinRepresentations 176–177 says "The dictionary between one-dimensional Artin representations and finite-order Hecke characters is read through `globalArtinMap`", and 294 forms "`det ρ ∘ globalArtinMap`, a finite-order `HeckeCharacter F`". The README's `globalArtinMap` is the finite-level map `C_K → Gal(L/K)^ab` for a chosen `L` (Layer 11: "For a finite Galois extension `L/K`, transport the abstract Artin equivalence"); it states no absolute `C_K → G_K^ab`, no continuity, and nothing connecting a character of `Gal(L/K)` composed with it to GlobalNumberFields' `HeckeCharacter` (whose conductor ArtinRepresentations 932 then equates with `characterConductorExp`). Only the local Layer 7 has the absolute form.
- PadicExtensionFamilies 58 and CurvesOverFiniteFields 285 use NewtonPolygons Convention 2 with the rational normalisation `v(q) = 1` for `q = p^r`; Layer 1's §1.2 ("the uniquely normalised rational-valued additive valuation" at a specified element) is not on main, and both drafts instead write `v_p / r` by hand. Not a defect in the README, but the normalisation is demand that no item above delivers, because the drafts never name §1.2.
- **Finite-idele description of the narrow class group** [HilbertModularForms line 709]. The draft needs "the finite-idele description of the narrow class group (GlobalNumberFields Layer 4)" to prove `GL₂⁺(F) \ GL₂(𝔸_{F,f}) / K₀(𝔫) ≃ NarrowClassGroup F`. GlobalNumberFields Layer 4 states only "Give the finite-idele description of `ClassGroup (𝓞 K)`: quotient by the everywhere-integral units and principal finite ideles" [README line 418], which is landed [`quotientEquivClassGroup`]. The narrow version, the quotient of the finite ideles by the integral units and the principal finite ideles of totally positive elements, is stated in no merged README; the ingredients are Tau Ceti's `NarrowClassGroup` [TauCeti/NumberTheory/NumberField/NarrowClassGroup/Basic.lean:84] and Layer 7's `IdeleCongruenceSubgroup (narrowModulus K)`.
- **Class numbers of orders in CM extensions of a real quadratic field** [HilbertModularSurfaces 3.4, lines 578–586, and table row 113]. The draft sums `h(S)` over orders `S` in the CM quartic fields `K/F` and cites ImaginaryQuadraticClassNumber H1.4 for "the same conductor formula over ℚ". H1.4 states the formula only for `O_f = ℤ + f𝓞_K` in an imaginary quadratic `K`; GlobalNumberFields Layer 11 gives `Pic S` finite for every order [`finite_pic`] but no count. The relative conductor formula `h(S)` for an `R`-order `S` in a CM extension of `F` is stated in no merged README.
- **Zariski closure of a subgroup of the rational points as a closed subgroup scheme** [SatoTateGroups lines 215–216 and 1268–1270]. The draft consumes from ReductiveGroups Layer 3 "the Zariski closure of a subgroup of the rational points as a closed subgroup scheme" and uses it for `G_ℓ^{Zar}`, "the Zariski closure of `ρ(Gal(k̄/k))` in `GL(V_ℓ)` as an algebraic group over `ℚ_ℓ`". Layer 3 states three bullets: "Hopf ideals ↔ closed subgroup schemes", "Normality and quotients", and "Identity component `G°` and component group `π₀(G)`" [README lines 235–247]; the only "Zariski" in the whole README is the closure of a morphism's image in the `A²∖{0}` computation at line 1895. The closed subgroup scheme generated by, or the Zariski closure of, an abstract subgroup of `G(k)` is stated nowhere.
- **The complex Dirichlet character `χ_D` with `NeZero |D|`** [HilbertModularSurfaces H2.2 use]. Not a README gap: IQCN H2.1 states it. Recorded here only because the two in-flight PRs [#19643 on the integer-valued character, #20772 on a general-ring `kroneckerChar` for ThetaSeries] build it under two names, and the merger should expect `fundamental-discriminant-char` to be closed by neither alone.
- The LMFDB `lattice.mass` and `lattice.class_number` columns rest on an exact mass, which is the Conway–Sloane formula 7H, and rank-16/rank-24 completeness is 7I; IntegralLattices says "The mass formula itself is not a milestone of this roadmap … Milestones 7B, 7D, 7F, 7H and 7I are therefore that successor's" and names `OrthogonalTamagawaAndLatticeMass`, which exists on no branch [the 2026-10-07 audit calls it "unwritten"]. 9B's `mass_isGenusMass` hypothesis therefore has no discharging supplier for any LMFDB record, and the `class_number = 24` of `lattice.24.1.1.24.1` and `= 2` of the rank-16 genus cannot be certified from merged READMEs.
- SiegelModularForms:609 wants "Minkowski reduction of positive definite real Y [hᵀYh ≥ y_{kk} for primitive h …] consuming the integral quadratic forms and lattices roadmap Layer 2"; IntegralLattices 2G states "Minkowski-reduced bases exist. A reduced Gram matrix of given rank and determinant has entries bounded …" for integral lattices, and Tau Ceti's `IsMinkowskiReduced` is a predicate on a ℤ-basis of an `IntegralLattice`. Reduction of a real positive definite matrix, with Siegel's fundamental-domain inequalities, is stated by no merged README.
- LMFDBLabelsAndCompleteness consumes the `lattice.genus_label` encoding ["j_1 … j_k are corresponding rank decompositions of their Jordan forms … encoded in base 62 … a hexadecimal string whose bits represent the local data"]; IntegralLattices 3E/3G state the Conway–Sloane 2-adic and genus symbols, not this encoding, and no merged README states it.
- LMFDB displays `lattice.spinor_genus` and the audit lists "spinor genus" among the lattice columns; IntegralLattices 4C ["The spinor genus spn L and the proper spinor genus spn⁺ L"] rests on 4B, whose stabilizers need Orthogonal and Spin Groups Layers 0, 2, 3 and Restricted Products — areas in no phase-1 group. Not itemized here for that reason.
- HilbertModularForms:1068 cites "Theta Series roadmap for the Petersson-kernel convergence inputs [Layer 3]"; Theta Series Layer 3 proves convergence of `thetaSeries L` on ℍ for a lattice in a real inner product space and states nothing about a Petersson kernel on a Hilbert modular surface. Whatever convergence lemma the draft needs is not a Theta Series milestone.
